# Decision log — rp-2026-10-08 (codename; statistics × AI, topic open)

The single source of truth. Newest entries at the bottom. Each entry states: **what was decided or found**, the **evidence** (a link), and **who decided**. Keep evidence, inference and proposal separate.

## 2026-10-08 — Stage 1 brief drafted: compute check and venue policy table

**Found (measured):** on MPS in bf16, forward throughput about 4.2k tok/s (Llama-3.2-1B), 3.0k (Qwen2.5-1.5B), 1.6k (Llama-3.2-3B) at sequence 256; generation 38 / 29 / 15 tok/s at batch 1 and 268 / 197 / 111 tok/s at batch 8; 3.2–7.2 GB of MPS memory. Script `tools/mps_bench.py`; logs `outputs/compute_check_2026-10-08/`. Offline loading of the Qwen and Llama-1B caches failed under transformers 5.17 until hub access was allowed (no weights were downloaded; the snapshots were unchanged).

**Found (venues, primary pages, 2026-10-08):** every venue within the three-month horizon (self-publication, arXiv, TMLR, ICML 2027, UAI 2027) permits LLM-assisted writing with full author responsibility and disclosure; TMLR expects ideas, claims and results to be human-sourced and a first-page footnote; ICLR's policy is the strictest disclosure model; Taylor & Francis journals require a tool-name-and-version disclosure and some restrict AI to language improvement; no IMS/AOAS generative-AI author policy was found (search-level). arXiv: endorsement needed (institutional email no longer sufficient since 2026-01-21); a reported May 2026 one-year ban for unchecked AI text rests on secondary sources only. AISTATS 2027 deadline passed; ICML 2027, UAI 2027 and COLM 2027 calls not yet published (dates are third-party projections). Table with links: [`PROJECT_BRIEF.md`](PROJECT_BRIEF.md).

**Proposal (not yet decided):** route = venue-neutral A6 paper, self-publication baseline, TMLR first external choice, arXiv if endorsed; prose = Claude drafts, Yichen revises and reads every word, AI-use disclosure to ICLR's standard.

Evidence: [`research/PROJECT_BRIEF.md`](PROJECT_BRIEF.md), [`outputs/compute_check_2026-10-08/`](../outputs/compute_check_2026-10-08/).

## 2026-10-08 — Stage 3 gates: C07 PASS, C05 FAIL (verdicts presented; Yichen to approve or pivot)

**Done:** arXiv terms checked ([`research/literature/TERMS_OF_USE_CHECK.md`](literature/TERMS_OF_USE_CHECK.md)); 42 e-prints fetched paced with a hashed manifest ([`research/literature/pdf_manifest.json`](literature/pdf_manifest.json)) plus 5 files reused from the previous project; 39 full texts read (8 delegated readers with page-confirmed quotations in `research/literature/notes/`, the decisive passages re-read by grep in this session, my own notes in `notes/_own_reading_C07.md`); the `arenakit` README and committed LMArena audit read through the GitHub API.

**Found, C07 (key-conditional validity of randomised detection tests):** the question was named as open by Jovanović et al. (arXiv 2506.16349, p.27, October 2025: "to the best of our knowledge not explored before … a much higher FPR than stated theoretically" for a subdomain) and measured once in an ICLR 2026 appendix (Gloaguen et al., arXiv 2509.24368, p.21: over 100 keys, Unigram's worst-key FPR 17.0% at the 1% level, key-to-key SD 1.2%, falling to 1.1% and 0.1% at context size 2); the mechanism is stated in words by Fernandez et al. (arXiv 2512.16904, p.19) who screen keys by a KS test; every theory paper (Li et al., Cai et al., Huang et al., Kuditipudi et al., the three e-process papers) states Type I control marginally over the key with one key in experiments; per-key calibration exists as practice (Aremu et al.; implicitly Xie et al.); the statistics of conditional validity (Vovk 2012; Bian & Barber 2023; Liang & Barber 2025) supply the frame. **Residual:** the theory (a dispersion law in √(n·C_h), the collision probability of the tuple law), its measurement across statistics, lengths, corpora and subdomains, and remedies with guarantees and costs. **Verdict: PASS** as a transfer-and-formalisation contribution; named scoop risk (the ETH/Meta group; the Su group). Gate: [`research/KEYCOND_GATE_v0.1.md`](KEYCOND_GATE_v0.1.md).

**Found, C05 (Bradley–Terry adequacy on arena votes):** Bradley–Terry is already formally rejected on 1,493,621 arena battles (Tang, Wang, Jin 2025, p.6); the arena's own pipeline deploys sandwich intervals (Chiang et al. 2024, pp.4–5); `arenakit` (MIT, 2026-08-07) probes ties, cycles, clustering and position on the 100k release with a public case study ("one strength per model is enough"); heterogeneity, fragility, rank certificates, conformal Elo, the winner's curse and tie-model extensions (Ameli et al., ICLR 2025) are all occupied. The residual (a classical specification battery with session-level homogeneity) is a paragraph, not a paper. **Verdict: FAIL / no-go.** Gate: [`research/ARENA_BT_GATE_v0.1.md`](ARENA_BT_GATE_v0.1.md).

**Proposal (Yichen decides):** lock C07 as the research question (TOPIC LOCK), then rename the folder and repository (Part C); alternative: pivot to the reserve C12 and gate it.

Evidence: the two gate files; `research/literature/notes/`.

## 2026-10-09 — R01 round 1 (Codex: REVISE, 11 findings) verified and answered; corrections to the gate, the brief and the data plan; three decisions for Yichen

**Review received:** [`research/reviews/R01_topic-lock-and-data-plan/REVIEW_codex_r1.md`](reviews/R01_topic-lock-and-data-plan/REVIEW_codex_r1.md) (baseline `8a71845`; F1, F4, F6 blocking; F2, F3, F5, F7–F10 major; F11 minor). **Verified by the conductor against the primary evidence** before acting (the table in [`RESPONSE_claude_r1.md`](reviews/R01_topic-lock-and-data-plan/RESPONSE_claude_r1.md)): every quotation and page the reviewer re-checked holds; its factual corrections are confirmed; the three sources it found were fetched, hashed and read at the cited pages (OpenAI's textGrain report of 2026-10-05, p.6; Delbianco, Tohmé and Acciarri, arXiv 2610.06317, pp.10–11; Gloaguen et al., arXiv 2602.06754, p.4).

**Corrections (the conductor's errors, recorded without rewriting earlier entries):**
1. The v0.1 gate's "no theory" for Jovanović et al. 2025 was wrong: p.27, Eq. 9 gives the hypergeometric finite-vocabulary special case. "Dispersion is not measured" for Fernandez et al.'s Table 6 was too strong: it measures key sensitivity at the sampling noise of a 100-text mean. Vovk pp.3–6 (PAC-type Propositions 2a/2b, tolerance regions) were under-used. The Stage 2 screen's "no retrieved work treats the per-key FPR as its object" was superseded by the Stage 3 appendix findings; the gate's "uncharacterised and unremedied" overstated the gap; "one key in experiments" holds only for the verified cases. The brief's "closed-form dispersion law" read as a delivered result where it was a proposal.
2. The v0.1 derivation used the independent-colour variance γ(1−γ)C_h for KGW's fixed-size split (the exact form has a finite-population correction, zero under a uniform token law at h = 0), dropped the conditional variance σ_k in the level approximation, applied token-law weights to the deduplicated comparator (the repair induces occupancy weights), and stated length and width effects as laws (now hypotheses with conditions; the across-key FPR is bounded). The calibration rule "N ≫ 1/C_h" was wrong: per-key calibration sample size follows tolerance-region and conformal theory (for a max-score threshold, (1−α)^N; N = 299 at α = 1%, 95%); the tuple law enters through the transfer error across text laws.
3. The audit draft derived "≈ 3,500 C4 documents per shard" from the datasets-server's partial count (1,809,584); the card gives 13,799,838 train documents (≈ 26,950 per shard). The oa-stackexchange file list had not been enumerated (14 parquet files under `data/`).
4. "What can be over-fitted is the text, not the key" was wrong: statistics, hash constructions and screening rules can adapt to benchmark seeds; confirmatory keys are reserved.
5. The brief's 24-hour run budget was an unlabelled downrating of the measured rates (now labelled; the null side's CPU cost is untimed and is timed at Stage 5 validation).

**Done:** [`research/KEYCOND_GATE_v0.2.md`](KEYCOND_GATE_v0.2.md) (v0.1 kept): the derivation rewritten as a hierarchy with exact identities and hypotheses H-length, H-width, H-domain, H-transfer; theorem targets T1–T3; three competitor rows added and three corrected; wording constraints 8–12; the verdict restated as PASS for an adequacy check plus a transfer plus a small method, pending Yichen's confirmation. [`PROJECT_BRIEF.md`](PROJECT_BRIEF.md) §Research question: target with the conditioning set, comparators' guarantees by method, positioning and study plan narrowed as a Stage 5 proposal, compute note labelled. [`corpora/CORPORA_AUDIT_v0.1.md`](corpora/CORPORA_AUDIT_v0.1.md) revised: the Common Pile v0.1 sources censused (news 126,673 documents with per-document CC BY/CC BY-SA; StackExchange threads with per-document CC BY-SA and a site field; Project Gutenberg public-domain books), the proceed rule on open, deduplicated, eligible units with a confirmatory floor and a pre-declared expansion order, the holdout with cross-source lineage grouping, reserved keys and a lock-bound seal, the null-text target restated as key-independent. `tools/census_hub.py` records the viewer's partial flags and the Common Pile sources. Llama-3.2 gated access confirmed by reading the gated licence file's metadata with the logged-in token (no download). Literature manifest: 2610.06317, 2602.06754 (paced) and the textGrain PDF (single fetch; terms line added).

**Waiting on Yichen (one at a time):** D1 positioning (confirm the narrowed contribution; recommended), D2 source set (option 1, fully open-content, no C4; recommended), D3 holdout (option A revised; recommended). Then round 2 to Codex.

**Decision D1 (Yichen, 2026-10-09):** the narrowed contribution (option 1 of 3, the recommended one): an adequacy check plus a transfer plus a small method, a theorem claimed only if one of the gate's targets T1–T3 is delivered; alternatives were re-gating C12 or keeping the theory-led framing. Gate v0.2's status line updated.

**Decision D2 (Yichen, 2026-10-09):** source set option 1 (recommended): Common Pile news_filtered, Wikipedia 20231101 in en/de/ru/ar/zh/ja, Common Pile stackexchange_filtered (two shards), Common Pile project_gutenberg_filtered (two shards); no C4, no WikiText-103, no HC3; no exception to the openly-licensed rule. Alternatives were option 2 (C4 under a logged exception for one comparability cell) and option 3 (the original list with two exceptions). Audit §3 and P1 updated.

**Decision D3 (Yichen, 2026-10-09):** holdout option A (recommended): a sealed 20% of units in every source and language by identifier hash after cross-source duplicate clustering, reserved confirmatory keys, a lock-bound seal with logged openings; alternatives were B (English sources only) and C (one whole source sealed). Written into the brief's holdout rule and the audit §5. **R01 round 2:** Yichen runs Codex with the prompt in `RESPONSE_claude_r1.md` §Next.

## 2026-10-09 — R01 round 2 (Codex: REVISE; F12–F19, with F14 and F15 blocking) verified and answered; no decision reopened

**Review received:** [`REVIEW_codex_r2.md`](reviews/R01_topic-lock-and-data-plan/REVIEW_codex_r2.md) (baseline `769332a`). **Verified** (table in [`RESPONSE_claude_r2.md`](reviews/R01_topic-lock-and-data-plan/RESPONSE_claude_r2.md)): every claim confirmed, including by exact enumeration of the reviewer's three counterexamples (the fixed-size variance identity and its reduction 3/11; the occupancy collision rising from 1/6 to 5/27 with length; two null laws with identical occupancy weights whose calibrated false-positive rates differ by 1/2 for two of six keys).

**Corrections (the conductor's errors):** (1) the round-1 response said the Qwen Instruct failure was "not found" in the compute logs; it is on lines 1–3 of `llama3b_offline.log` (the conductor had grepped only the other log); corrected in place with a dated note. (2) The gate's round-1 derivation still used γ where the realised green fraction g = m/V belongs, left C_cond undefined, claimed that the occupancy collision falls with length and that the retained set tends to the vocabulary (both false in general), evaluated the density at the wrong point in the linearisation, called serial dependence variance-increasing, stated the calibration identity without its continuity and strict-rejection conditions, and premised the transfer target T2 on the mean-shift term, which controls the difference of means only. All corrected in gate v0.2 §Residual gap (round-2 changelog); T2 is now a score-tail discrepancy statement; a theorem is claimed only if T1 or T2 is proved. (3) The audit called the Common Pile's Dolma JSON-lines shards "Parquet" and based P5 on Parquet footers; formats, paths, sizes and the reconciliation rule corrected. (4) The holdout specification lacked the cluster identity and representative rule, the order of expansion and assignment, cluster-level forcing of the exposure record and the quarantine rule; P7's fallback removed its floor and two Gutenberg shards could not meet it (≈ 1,479 held-out books); all fixed (mandatory cells, binding floor, four Gutenberg shards, capped expansion with a FAIL outcome). (5) The terms check lacked the Common Pile sources (three rows added, superseded rows marked); the audit promised a uniform vintage schema that Gutenberg cannot supply (per-source schema; "unknown" for Gutenberg; "pre-LLM by a century" withdrawn). ## 2026-10-10 — Milestone 2: Study 1 run under the lock, analysis, record and report v0.1; checkpoint R04 opened

**Done (no decision needed; Yichen's "lock and run" of 2026-10-10 covers the run):** pre-run checks (tree clean and pushed at `efa548c`; `verify_lock` OK; no cells existed; 1,248 cells expected, 54 markers); the run `PYTHONHASHSEED=0 s1_run.py --grid research/study1/grid_v0.3.json` from 03:26:16 to 04:55:50 UTC (1.49 h against the 2.01 h projection; one start line; no chain skipped; launched at 1.9 GB of swap in use with other applications open, recorded in the manifest); the locked analyzer with the lock → `outputs/study1_v0.3/results.json`; `research/study1/s1_manifest.py` (new) rebuilt `RUN_MANIFEST.json` from the run log, the cost records, the lock (verified a third time) and the environment; the record committed and pushed as `62aa6ea` (1,248 cells and 42 cost records, `run.log`, `results.json`, the manifest; 66 MB tracked in all). The report builder `research/study1/s1_report.py` and its prose template were written during the run and committed before any outcome was read (`73868aa`); the builder re-computes R1–R5 from the per-cell files with its own code (the key-mean and SD from the recorded per-key levels, the two-sided KL bound, the known-mean estimator and the 6·SE + τ rule, the R1 statuses and counts, the chain monotonicity, the R4b limits) and asserts equality with `results.json`; it was exercised on the disclosed pilot in a fixture mode that can write only outside the repository (its fixture figures were viewed for layout after the lock; no pilot number is reported anywhere), then run in study mode: every re-computation equals `results.json`. [`STUDY1_REPORT_v0.1.md`](STUDY1_REPORT_v0.1.md) is generated (every number a filled placeholder; 15 tables; 5 figures with the dataviz palette validated and each figure viewed; `outputs/study1_v0.3/report/`).

**Results (the pre-registered rules of protocol §9; details in the report's Table 1):** R1 **PASS** (360 candidates; 180 eligible; 152 resolved and all 152 consistent; 28 unresolved, every one at h = 0; the observed-to-predicted ratio 0.79–1.20 over the resolved cells); R2 **PASS** (891 checks, 0 flagged, smallest two-sided KL bound 0.27); R3 **PASS** (1,098 checks, 0 gross failures, nominal-95% coverage 94.3%); the pre-registered statement "the fixed-size reduction is below 1% for V ≥ 10,000" **refuted** (largest 28.3%; 45 of the 180 distinct-rule cells at or above 1%, all at h ≤ 1; every raw-rule and first-rule cell below 1%); R4 **PASS** (40 of 40 raw-rule chains non-decreasing; descriptively also 40 of 40 under the distinct and first rules); R4b **reproduced** (ρ̄ = 1.0000 independent and 0.9510 fixed at L = 1,024 against the analytic limits); R5 **PASS** (48 of 48 chains). The refuted statement stays refuted as pre-registered.

**Found (labelled unanticipated in the report's §5; inference in §6):** the refuted statement's mechanism is the gate's own saturation (residual-gap item 3): deduplication flattens the occupancy weights over the covered vocabulary (1/C_cond = 2,831 of V = 10,000 at h = 0, L = 2,048), so the correction's size is rule-dependent and the gate's "of order 1/(V·C_cond)" holds for raw tuple laws only; the identity itself holds in those cells (exact ratio 0.717 against an observed 0.667 ± 0.080). The 28 unresolved R1 cells are all at h = 0 with point ratios below 1; the pairwise Gaussian predictor gives the same ratios as the common-factor one (so not heterogeneity), and the one key sample shared by every cell sits 0.4–2.2 normal SEs below its exact binomial key-mean across the 36 h = 0 distinct-rule cells (one correlated draw). At α = 0.001 three short h = 0 cells are inconsistent (ratio ≈ 0.5): the normal residual approximation fails in the far tail where a few heavy tokens dominate a 64-token text. The raw rule at h = 0 gives a nearly binary per-key level (across-key SD 42–49 pp at L = 2,048; the worst sampled key at 100%). At h ≥ 3 the dispersion sits at the finite-text floor (0.157 pp at N = 4,000) on these laws. Timing 1.49 h against 2.01 h projected.

**Corrections (the conductor's errors, this session; nothing in a pinned file changed after the lock):** builder bugs fixed before the study-mode build (a cell's file name not attached on loading; the descriptive block read from the wrong level of `results.json`; a constant used before its definition); figure layout faults found on the fixture render and fixed (colliding panel titles in Figures 1 and 5; overlapping y-labels in Figure 3); in the first study-mode render the fixed-subset table lacked a γ column (the two γ printed as duplicate rows) and was moved to a full file with a condensed body table; a level printed without its percent sign; four wording faults in the filled sections (a degenerate range, a signed deviation, a wrong denominator, an overstatement about h ≥ 3 cells) fixed in the template and the builder.

**Checkpoint R04 (the Study 1 report) reached:** prompt in [`reviews/R04_study1-report/PROMPT.md`](reviews/R04_study1-report/PROMPT.md); the orientation's §2 and §7 updated. **Waiting on Yichen:** the Codex run of R04. Nothing verdict-dependent proceeds (no Study 2 protocol work beyond reading).

**Skill updated (A5):** (a) when a pre-registered statement is refuted, check whether its derivation silently assumed a premise that another part of the same theory removes (here the raw-law collision structure against the deduplicated occupancy weights); (b) a grid that shares one key sample across every cell makes cell-wise "systematic" deviations a single correlated draw, to be said before a pattern is read into them.

## 2026-10-10 — R04 round 1 (Codex: REVISE; F1–F6 major, F7–F10 minor) verified and answered; report v0.1 regenerated

**Review received:** [`reviews/R04_study1-report/REVIEW_codex_r1.md`](reviews/R04_study1-report/REVIEW_codex_r1.md) (baseline `58ab64e`). The reviewer reproduced the lock digests, the inventory, every rule count, the analyzer's output byte for byte and the report's assembly, and found the problems in the interpretation and the presentation. **Verified** (table in [`RESPONSE_claude_r1.md`](reviews/R04_study1-report/RESPONSE_claude_r1.md)): every claim confirmed against the cells, `results.json`, the locked analyzer at `b994105` and the rendered report; none disputed; no judgement call for Yichen.

**Corrections (the conductor's errors, this round; nothing in a locked or record file changed):**
1. **(F2) The Milestone 2 entry's reading of the refuted statement was wrong.** It said the gate's "of order 1/(V·C_cond)" holds for raw tuple laws only, with C_cond the tuple collision C_h. The gate defines C_cond = A/B for the weights actually scored and its identity and order expression are general; for an i.i.d. raw law A/B equals the unigram collision C_0 at every width (A/B = 0.01717 against C_0 = 0.01721 and C_h = 2.948e-4 on the Zipf 10,000, h = 1, L = 2,048 raw cell). What the pre-registered numerical statement assumed was C_cond ≈ C_0 (then about 0.58% at V = 10,000); under the distinct rule the deduplicated occupancy weights make 1/C_cond approach the covered support (2,831 of 10,000 at L = 2,048), hence 28.3%. The statement is refuted as registered; the identity is not; the paper must keep A/B, C_h and Ã(h) distinct. The earlier entry stands as history.
2. **(F3) "The dispersion at h ≥ 3 sits at the finite-text floor" was overstated.** 140 of 144 candidate cells at h ≥ 3 are ineligible (theory share below 0.75), but four h = 3 cells are eligible and consistent (theory share 0.76–0.89; SD 0.32–0.49 pp against the 0.157 pp Bernoulli benchmark); no h = 4 cell is eligible. The "invisible at h ≥ 3", "far more than 4,000 texts" and "real text's collisions decay more slowly" claims are withdrawn; the last is Study 2's question.
3. **(F4) The attribution was over-stated.** "Heterogeneity is not the cause", "the pairwise predictor never improves", "normality is the binding approximation", "within ±20% in every resolved cell" (resolved ratios reach 0.7918 and 1.2047) and "matches the exact binomial everywhere" are withdrawn; the report now separates the registered pass, the descriptive range and a suggestive account with both pairwise subsamples (73 of 180 primary cells have the pairwise point ratio closer to 1; the V = 10 fixed illustration cell is markedly better under the pairwise form), states errors as prediction/observation factors (1.25–1.43 at α = 1% in the four short raw-rule cells; 1.97–2.09 at α = 0.001) and says "no check triggered the registered audit" for R2 with its power limits.
4. **(F1) R4b's locked analyzer applies 3·SE + 10⁻⁹ where the protocol's text says 3·SE.** The four longest-length residuals are 1e-14 to 1e-13 against 3·SE of 1e-16 to 1e-14, so the literal text fails in all four while the implemented rule passes; the tolerance predates the lock (`b994105`), the verdict stands as the locked rule's, and the discrepancy is disclosed in the report (§5; Table 8b) and here. A protocol's text must state every tolerance its code applies (skill lesson).
5. **(F6) A priority phrase** ("what is new here is its per-key face") violated gate constraints 13 and 16; replaced by "measured here on these synthetic laws" with the named record.
6. **(F8, F10) Builder faults:** the unit-σ failures' σ² read from the unit-σ block (1.00) instead of the cells (0.742, 0.715); "they enter no rule" for the two flagged V = 10 cells, which R3 treats as structural zeros; the growth factors unlabelled as h = 1; the fixed colouring's within-chain pairing caveat missing; literal outcome-sized assertions in the template; image links relative to the repository root instead of the report; unescaped pipes in table cells; Figure 5's title collision reintroduced by a title edit made after the last viewing; the "(b) every live cell" caption (1,185 of 1,194 plotted). (ii) Found while verifying: "for the raw rule at h = 0 when σ_δ is below about 0.8" was wrong as a boundary (consistent raw cells at h = 0 have σ_δ from 0.995 to 11.1; the unresolved four are the shortest cells, M = 64–128, of the heavier laws).

**Done:** the builder and the prose template corrected (F1–F10 and the two further corrections); `tables_r1_diagnostics_full.md` (212 non-consistent occurrences over 141 cells, both pairwise subsamples); Tables 4, 8, 8b, 14 extended; the process-and-disclosure paragraph generated from the record (conductor, reviewer, Yichen's responsibility, the pilot namespaces, the post-lock fixture use, the R04 status read from the reviews folder); Figures 2–5 regenerated and viewed; the report regenerated (the independent re-computation again equal to `results.json`; every table's rows checked against its header; links checked). The orientation's §7 and the handoff updated. **Skill updated (A5):** the 2026-10-10 lesson reworded and extended (the gate's identity was general and the numerical statement assumed C_cond ≈ C_0; state every tolerance in the protocol text; a generated report needs a rendered-Markdown check, links relative to the report, escaped pipes, and a re-view after every figure edit).

**Checkpoint R04 round 2:** Yichen runs Codex with the prompt in `PROMPT.md` §Round 2. **Waiting on Yichen:** that run.

## 2026-10-12 — R05 round 1 (Codex: REVISE; F1–F3 major, F4–F8 minor) verified and answered

**Review received:** [`reviews/R05_study2-protocol/REVIEW_codex_r1.md`](reviews/R05_study2-protocol/REVIEW_codex_r1.md) (baseline `54a2c65`). The reviewer reproduced the pools, draws and digests, the 544/217 eligibility counts, the S2 thresholds, the projection to the second (6.264 h at D8's setting), the closed-form cross-check at all 6,120 KGW cell/alpha combinations of the inputs (largest difference 2.1e-13), the pre-outcome audit over 2,019 retained experiment files (no record starting in 22,000–22,999), and found the problems in the inventory gate, the record contract and the language block. **Verified** (table in [`RESPONSE_claude_r1.md`](reviews/R05_study2-protocol/RESPONSE_claude_r1.md)): every claim reproduced against the code and the retained pilot cells before any change; none disputed; no judgement call for Yichen (the design, D7 and D8 stand; the reviewer concurs with K = 1,000).

**Corrections (the conductor's errors, this round; no frozen input changed in content, no Study 1 file touched):**
1. **(F1) The analyzer's inventory gate could lose a live cell silently.** A cell lacking `N_eff`, or carrying `N_eff = 0`, passed the gate and was then excluded from every rule by the `live` predicate (reproduced: 66 files accepted, 65 live). Now `s2_run.validate_cell_body` is a content contract applied by the analyzer and by the runner's reuse check: N_eff must equal N (every production window has L > h and every rule retains at least one tuple per text), every required field must be present, a marker or flagged cell at a live slot is refused.
2. **(F2) The level counts were length-checked only.** Counts of −1, 0.5, N_eff + 1 and an all-zero vector contradicting the stored summary were accepted (reproduced). The contract now requires K integers in [0, N_eff], recomputes the key mean, sample SD, quantiles, extrema, exceedance shares and KL bound from the counts and reconciles them with the stored summaries within 10⁻⁹, and requires the counts to be nested across α; the study-mode lock check now precedes the inventory listing, so the no-lock fixture reaches its own branch.
3. **(F3) The language block used seven pools and called all-zero keys infinitely disparate.** The selection `startswith("wikipedia_")` included wikipedia/en@1024 (reproduced: seven tags), and a zero minimum gave an infinite ratio counted as "> 2" and "> 5" even when every level was zero (reproduced: share 1.0 on all-zero keys). Now the six @256 Wikipedia cells are selected explicitly from the grid, and three pre-stated cases are counted per key (all zero; some zero with some positive; all positive, over which alone the ratio's shares, median and maximum are computed, with that denominator stated).
4. **(F4) The crossed bootstrap omitted the 1% key quantile** the protocol promised; added, and the analyzer now asserts every promised key of the crossed block on every deployer-subset cell.
5. **(F5) "With 5 text-bootstrap resamples only the text-bootstrap SEs change" was false:** the text bootstrap consumes the cell's random stream before the key bootstrap, the pairwise subsample and the two-way bootstrap, so those Monte Carlo realisations differ (the estimands do not). The claim and the fixture are narrowed to the deterministic quantities, and the fixture's chain is named for what it is (N = 600, L = 256 at the real vocabulary size, not a production-sized chain).
6. **(F6) The 200-node Gauss–Hermite quadrature could not integrate the indicator at σ = 0** (0.0130 against the exact 0.0100 at a unit shift variance; the same at σ² = 10⁻⁶). The Gaussian-shift predictor with per-text thresholds is now a closed form (`mixture_gaussian_closed`: the convolution mean and the bivariate normal orthant probability through Owen's T over the distinct thresholds), equal to Study 1's closed form to 0.0 at σ² ∈ {0, 10⁻⁶, 10⁻³, 0.5, 1} and to the quadrature to 3e-16 at an ordinary σ²; the quadrature remains only for the discrete simulated-shift sample, where direct averaging is the intended operation. The primary KGW decision was never affected (it uses Study 1's closed form); the Gumbel predictions in `INPUTS.json` were regenerated with the closed form.
7. **(F7) Over-stated precision prose.** The normal-SD benchmark is optimistic for nearly Bernoulli per-key levels (relative SE about 15.6% at p = 0.01, K = 1,000); S2's dead-detector sentence now states the exact-μ condition and the counts from the key-free inputs (20 of 680 KGW distinct-rule cells and no Gumbel cell at α = 0.01 at N = 10,000); "any dependence" is qualified to texts and cells; the 95% intervals are called nominal, the power table an eligibility count with a normal benchmark; the report will show endpoint and tie counts and the number of keys in the upper tail per cell (now in the analyzer's cell summaries).
8. **(F8) Provenance and timing prose.** `INPUTS.json` carried the digest of the first grid (`cfc9849`, `85d7e963…`), the later grid differing only in the text-bootstrap count and note, which that computation does not use; the file is regenerated under the final grid and code (the reviewed file kept as `INPUTS_cfc9849grid_superseded.json`; a first regeneration, `INPUTS_quadrature_superseded.json`, still evaluated the Gumbel predictions by the quadrature because `s2_inputs.py` had not been switched, which a file comparison caught before the validator ran; every KGW quantity and every Gumbel moment are identical across the three files, and the final file's Gumbel predictions are the closed form's, item 6). The Milestone 3 "Done" paragraph of 2026-10-11 quoted the rates and peak memory of the earlier validation run (1.30e-8, 2.07e-9, 6.95 GB) where the retained run gives 1.33e-8, 2.11e-9 and a 5.05 GiB validator peak; the handoff's "about 7 GB" was that validator figure, not a runner measurement. Corrected here (the earlier paragraph stands as history); the validator now also runs the runner itself on a production-sized synthetic chain at K = 1,000 (the largest chain type, all five lengths) and records the runner's own peak memory and time against the projection of that chain type.

**Done:** the code and fixtures as above (`s2_core.py`, `s2_run.py`, `s2_analyze.py`, `s2_validate.py`); `INPUTS.json` regenerated; the validator re-run and §11 regenerated; the protocol's §§1, 3, 4, 7, 8, 9 revised (the simulated shift qualified as a moment-matched shape diagnostic; the closed form; the record's limits; the manifest's N "at least"; the language block; the Monte Carlo realisations; the rare-key precision; the body contract). The orientation's §7 and the handoff updated. **Checkpoint R05 round 2:** Yichen runs Codex with the prompt in `PROMPT.md` §Round 2. **Waiting on Yichen:** that run. No lock exists; no study cell exists.

## 2026-10-12 — R05 round 2 (Codex: REVISE; F9–F10 major, F11 minor) verified and answered

**Review received:** [`reviews/R05_study2-protocol/REVIEW_codex_r2.md`](reviews/R05_study2-protocol/REVIEW_codex_r2.md) (baseline `8a7d450`). F1–F8 closed (two residuals carried into F9 and F10); the closed form checked against independent integration (largest error 4.0e-16), the language block on every production slot (24 blocks of six languages), the eligibility counts, the projection, the probe-scaled 8.1 h, the byte-identical token manifest, the regenerated inputs (the only numerical change the Gumbel predictions' evaluation, at most 2.1e-11) and the untouched Study 1 record all reproduced. **Verified** (table in [`RESPONSE_claude_r2.md`](reviews/R05_study2-protocol/RESPONSE_claude_r2.md)): every finding reproduced on a fresh synthetic pilot before any change; none disputed; no judgement call for Yichen.

**Corrections (the conductor's errors, this round; no frozen input, no Study 1 file and no scientific constant changed):**
1. **(F9) The body contract was fail-open on non-finite values and trusted flags.** A stored NaN KL bound passed the difference test (a NaN comparison is false), a false `structural_zero` flag removed a cell from S2 without refusal, and the order of the ratio and SD intervals and the finiteness of the SD bootstrap SE were unchecked, so S2 could pass on a cell that should have failed and S4/S5 tolerances could become NaN. The contract now requires the exact mean and the KL bound finite in [0, 1] and reconciled, the structural-zero flag equal to its derivation from the exact mean and the counts, a finite non-negative SE, finite ordered intervals with the ratio interval equal to the SD interval over a positive prediction (the registered non-finite exception only at a zero prediction), and exactly the five registered quantile keys; S2 recomputes the bound from the counts and derives the structural zero itself; refusal and acceptance fixtures added.
2. **(F10) The deployer's view read the stored flag.** A false or missing `deployer_subset` on one deployer cell was accepted and silently removed its three rows (33 of 36); a crossed block of null values and an empty quantile dictionary passed. The contract now requires the flag to equal the grid-derived membership, the deployer's view is selected from the grid, every crossed statistic must be a populated record (two finite ordered bounds in [0, 1], a finite non-negative SE, B = 200), and the validator asserts the pilot's deployer-row count against the grid; the runner's reuse check applies the same contract.
3. **(F11) A sentence of §9 contradicted the corrected calculation** ("no under-rejection is flaggable at nominal 0.01 or 0.001"): the 20 KGW distinct cells whose exact mean exceeds 0.01440 can be flagged for under-rejection at nominal 0.01 (gutenberg/en@1024, Llama, h = 4, L = 64, distinct, γ = 0.25: exact mean 0.015257, bound 4.2e-7 at an observed mean of 0). Corrected, with the nominal-versus-exact distinction stated; the 200-node quadrature constant labelled as the validation fixture's; the header date updated.

**Done:** the code and fixtures as above; the validator re-run and §11 regenerated; the protocol's §9 revised; `RESPONSE_claude_r2.md`; the round-3 prompt (bounded to F9–F11) in `PROMPT.md`. **Checkpoint R05 round 3:** Yichen runs Codex with the prompt in `PROMPT.md` §Round 3. **Waiting on Yichen:** that run. No lock exists; no study cell exists.

## 2026-10-13 — Study 2 run completed under the lock; analysed; the record committed; report v0.1 generated; checkpoint R06 opened

**The run:** 2026-10-13T13:45:36Z to 18:36:36Z (4.85 h against the validation's fitted 6.08 h and the probe-scaled 8.13 h; the fitted model over-projected the small @256 chains, ratios 0.46–0.89, and under-projected the longest by a fifth, 746 s against 622 s); 100 chains, 3,468 cells, 100 cost records; no restart, no resume, no error; peak RSS 5.41 GB; `DONE` in `outputs/study2_v0.1/run.log`. **Analysis under the lock:** `s2_analyze.py --grid research/study2/grid_v0.1.json --lock outputs/study2_v0.1/PRE_RUN_LOCK.json` → `results.json`; `s2_manifest.py` → `RUN_MANIFEST.json` (the lock verified a third time; the inventory complete and digested).

**Pre-registered outcomes (protocol §9):** **S1 PASS** (544 candidates; 217 eligible; 184 resolved; 178 consistent, 6 inconsistent, 33 unresolved; share 96.7% against 80% with ≥ 30 resolved); **S2 PASS** (3,060 checks, 0 flagged; smallest bound 1.00); **S3 PASS** (3,468 checks, 0 gross; coverage 94.8%); **S4 FAIL** (76 of 100 raw-rule chains non-decreasing in L; 24 failing: 18 of the 20 h = 4 chains, the six news/en@256 chains at h = 1–4 under Llama and h = 1, 4 under Qwen, and wikipedia/de and /ru under Llama at h = 1); **S5 PASS** (40 of 40). The report's §5 reads S4's failures as two regimes (the finite-text floor at h = 4, whose level follows the lattice size of the discrete z-test at γ = 0.25: 1.54% at M = 60, 1.02% at 124, 1.35% at 252; and the templated news prefix, where ρ̄ itself falls with L) — the reading, not a re-interpretation of the rule.

**Findings beyond the rules (§5, labelled):** the common-factor predictor under-predicts the across-key SD on real text by about a fifth at γ = 0.25, α = 0.01 (resolved ratios 0.833–1.78, median 1.21; every non-consistent cell above 1), by a median factor 1.72 at α = 0.001 (41.7% consistent) and not at all at γ = 0.5 (median 0.984, 100%) or α = 0.05 (1.08, 100%); neither the pairwise-covariance nor the simulated-shift predictor removes it (medians 1.20, 1.19), the unit-σ predictor is worse (57.4%); the Gumbel family's Gaussian-residual approximation is worse still (59.0%, median 1.33). The deployer's view at h = 1 and the longest length: raw rule key-mean 2.52–8.65%, 99% key 4.8–38.3%, worst sampled key 6.1–51.6%; distinct rule key-mean 1.09–1.18%, median key 0.92–1.13%, 99% key 1.9–3.7%, worst sampled key 2.2–6.6%, 0.5–10.3% of keys above 2α; at h = 0 the raw rule is nearly binary (99% key 37–96%; the worst sampled key over all KGW γ = 0.25 cells 99.4%). H-domain: a key's level correlates across the five English cells (Llama h = 0 raw median 0.83; h = 1 distinct 0.56) and not across languages (near 0); H-language: at h ≤ 1 most keys differ by more than a factor 2 across the six Wikipedia languages (h = 0 raw 99.8–100%, median ratio 16; h = 1 raw 75–76%, distinct 66–74%, median 2.3–2.5), at h = 2 the disparity is 1.6–2.8 times the finite-text floor; the tokenizer axis: Qwen2.5 tokens give 1.06–2.56 (raw) and 1.02–1.42 (distinct) times the Llama-3.2 dispersion at h = 1; the across-tokenizer correlation is largest at h = 0 (up to 0.93). The fixed-size reduction on real text at V ≥ 128k: raw ≤ 0.35%, first ≤ 0.14%, distinct up to 2.10% (7 of 68 cells ≥ 1%).

**Post-hoc side computation (disclosed in §5; not pre-registered; no key or level):** `research/study2/tokenizer_overlap.py`: only 410 of the 128,256 ids compared carry the same token string in both tokenizers (ids 0–409), yet those ids carry 6.9–35.4% (Llama) and 11.8–40.6% (Qwen) of the production token occurrences — the coinciding-ids mechanism the protocol named for the across-tokenizer correlation.

**Integrity:** the builder's re-computation equals `results.json` in every check (`report/checks.json`); the 9,180 (cell, α) predictions compared with the outcomes equal the pre-lock `INPUTS.json` values to 0.0; the fixture's disclosure and the unit-σ text/code discrepancy are in §1, §5 and Table 11. **Report:** `research/STUDY2_REPORT_v0.1.md` (21,680 words; 21 body tables, six supplements, six figures viewed); sections 5–8 are the conductor's labelled reading; §8 lists six open items for R06 (the reading of S1's bias and of S4's regimes as wording judgements for Yichen; the unit-σ discrepancy as a standing rule for the next protocol; the overlap computation and the Qwen hypothesis; Study 3's design inputs; the figures for the paper).

**Checkpoint R06 opened** (`research/reviews/R06_study2-report/PROMPT.md`; orientation updated with the Study 2 record row and the R06 row). Stage 6 exit for Study 2 follows R06's AGREE.

## 2026-10-13 — R06 round 1 (Codex: REVISE; F1–F7 major, F8 minor) verified and answered: every finding accepted; the report regenerated on the unpinned path

**Review received:** [`reviews/R06_study2-report/REVIEW_codex_r1.md`](reviews/R06_study2-report/REVIEW_codex_r1.md) (baseline `23ed55a`): the reviewer reproduced every registered verdict (S1 PASS, S2 PASS, S3 PASS, S4 FAIL, S5 PASS), the complete inventory, the 10,404 level blocks, the 9,180 pre-lock prediction comparisons, the chain projections, the correlation matrices, the language blocks and the lattice sizes, and found no reason to edit the locked protocol or rerun the grid; it asked for corrections on the unpinned report path. **Verified by the conductor before acting** (table in [`RESPONSE_claude_r1.md`](reviews/R06_study2-report/RESPONSE_claude_r1.md)): every alleged factual error confirmed by recomputation, none disputed.

**The conductor's corrections (all accepted; no locked file, record file or verdict changed; the report regenerated):** F1, the post-hoc tokenizer computation had divided the Qwen counts by the positions below 128,256 only (Russian 16.36% reported against 13.80% correct; Arabic 14.19% against 11.38%; Japanese 15.11% against 14.01%; the first output kept as `report/tokenizer_overlap_superseded_r1.json`) and had quoted the same-string share as the mechanism, where the hash is a function of the integer ids alone; the script now computes the key-free identity-predicted across-tokenizer correlation of q̂ from the document-averaged weights over hash tuple ids (the recomputed A equals every cell's stored identity) and compares it with the observed correlation of the stored per-key q̂ values: predicted 0.065–0.944 against observed 0.087–0.938 at h = 0 and 0.005–0.766 against −0.013–0.783 at h = 1, in agreement cell by cell; the shared-tuple weight share 14–93% at h = 0 and 0.7–23% at h = 1; the semantic share (11.4–40.6% under Qwen with the full denominator) is reported as a labelled diagnostic. F2, the H-domain prose rewritten by pair class and tokenizer ("every correlation is higher" was false for the English pairs: 0.544 under Qwen against 0.562 under Llama at h = 1 distinct; ja–zh 0.176 and 0.460); "transfers" replaced by the measured correlation statement, and within-English, cross-language and cross-tokenizer transfer listed as Study 3 hypotheses to be measured with the calibration-sample error. F3, the attribution of the predictor's bias to the per-text score model softened to a plausible account the study does not isolate; the median factors are descriptions, not correction factors; the Study 1 comparison states the different primary γ. F4, the registered outputs delivered: the 1% and 5% deployer quantiles with crossed intervals and K / N_eff in every table; the population diagnostic (Table 15b and a full supplement; on the 217 eligible primary cells the two-way / mixture variance ratio has median 1.56, beside S1's squared median SD ratio 1.46); the per-cell key-count supplement with the level-1 count added to the diagnostic rows; the unit-σ block's 101 non-consistent occurrences in the diagnostic supplement (548 rows); the six S1 counts by width. ## 2026-10-13 — Milestone 5 opened: Stage 5 for Study 3; decision D9 (scope)

**Session start (Part D):** no open review round (R01–R06 each carry a `RESOLUTION.md`); rename not pending. Read: the handoff, this log's tail, the Study 2 report §§5–8, the R05 and R06 carried-forward items, the brief's study plan, the gate's residual-gap items 5 and 7 and constraints 10, 13, 14, AutoMark §3.2, App. C and Algorithm 3 (local full text, `literature/txt/2609.37310.txt`).

**Decision D9 (Yichen, 2026-10-13): Study 3's scope — calibration and transfer on the null side** (the recommended option; the alternatives were the same plus a frequency-stratified fixed-size green list at h = 0 as a new method, and a narrow design with per-key conformal calibration against the guaranteed multiplier and within-English transfer only). Study 3 compares, on fresh null texts and fresh keys: the nominal test; AutoMark's guaranteed multiplier 1/β = 20 and its empirical multiplier fitted on a calibration law and carried to a deployment law (Gloaguen, Staab, Vechev 2026, Proposition 1 and App. C; gate constraint 14); per-key conformal calibration (expected level ≤ α under exchangeability); per-key tolerance (PAC) calibration (Vovk 2012: P(level > α) ≤ δ over the calibration draw). Unit: a key × a calibration law P′ × a deployment law P; thresholds set on N_cal texts of P′, the realised level measured on disjoint evaluation texts of P; every pair of cells within a tokenizer (the diagonal within-law, the off-diagonal transfer; cross-tokenizer secondary). A pre-registered transfer predictor from the key-free between-cell shift covariance identity (g(1−g)·Σ_u w_P(u) w_P′(u) under independent colouring; deferred from Study 2 §7). The alternative remedy is the retention rule and context width, read from the same runs (no new scheme). The cost is recorded on the null side as the threshold shift and the thresholds are frozen for Study 4's power side. Gate constraint 10 governs every transfer sentence. Next: D10 (settings), then D11 (sizes against a measured projection).

**Decision D10 (Yichen, 2026-10-13): Study 3's settings — the deployer grid** (the recommended option; the alternatives were the distinct rule only at h ∈ {1, 2}, and the deployer grid plus a length axis and the first rule). KGW γ = 0.25 (independent colouring) and Gumbel-max; raw and distinct rules; h ∈ {0, 1, 2}; L = 256 in all ten cells (the @1024 cells' prefix; books from token 1,000 as in Study 2); both tokenizers (transfer within each tokenizer; cross-tokenizer secondary); α = 0.01 primary, 0.05 and 0.001 secondary; β = δ = 0.05. 240 cell-settings (Study 2's deployer subset). The basis (Study 2 Table 14 at L = 256, distinct rule): the across-key SD 1.5–2.4 pp at h = 0, 0.28–0.68 pp at h = 1, 0.14–0.36 pp at h = 2, against a per-key calibration sampling SD of about √(α(1−α)/N_cal) (0.31 pp at N_cal = 1,000; 0.14 pp at 5,000; design arithmetic), so the grid spans the regimes where calibration should clearly pay off, break even, or lose to the nominal test. The two English Wikipedia cells (@256 and the @1024 prefix) serve as a near-identical-law control, with calibration and evaluation ids disjoint across every cell. Next: D11 (sizes).

**Fact (2026-10-13, metadata only; no text, no key):** fresh open eligible texts per cell after Study 2's 10,000 and every Study 2 id excluded: news/en@256 27,695; wikipedia/en@256 76,113; stackexchange/en@256 972,004; wikipedia/de 90,042; ru 52,249; ar 74,999; zh 106,578; ja 58,120; wikipedia/en@1024 23,332 (its whole pool lies inside the en@256 pool); **gutenberg/en@1024 2,122** (pool 12,122). Computed by a scratch script over `s2_data.eligible_pool` and the frozen Study 2 draws (every draw's seed and ids digest re-checked against `tokens_manifest.json`).

**Decision D11 (Yichen, 2026-10-13): sizes and calibration design — a shared calibration set per cell, Study 2's texts as the evaluation sets** (the recommended option; the alternatives were per-key calibration subsets from a 10,000-text fresh pool with Gutenberg deployment-only, and all-fresh texts on nine cells). K = 1,000 evaluation keys in a fresh range 23,000–23,999, and 1,000 disjoint keys 24,000–24,999 used only to fit the empirical multiplier; the evaluation set of every cell is Study 2's frozen 10,000 texts (tokens cached and verified by the Study 2 manifest; Gutenberg 7,122 of them); one shared calibration set of 5,000 fresh texts per cell, drawn as the next ids of the cell's Study 2 permutation with every id disjoint from every evaluation and calibration set of every cell (Gutenberg: its 2,122 fresh books plus 2,878 of its Study 2 texts, which then leave its evaluation set); nested N_cal ∈ {500, 1,000, 2,000, 5,000} (prefixes of the calibration set); a calibration-set bootstrap for the calibration-set component. The target is α_P(k, D_cal) with D_cal frozen (the brief's definition), the "standard public calibration corpus" scenario. Projection about 3 h by Study 2's fitted rates (the conductor's arithmetic; the validator measures it before the lock). Fresh keys make Study 2's texts admissible as evaluation sets (no outcome exists for these keys); the reuse also gives a replication check of Study 2's nominal dispersion on the same texts with independent keys. Next: the code, the protocol draft and the validation on inputs only (no decision needed until R07).

The error was the conductor's: a quadrature chosen without a convergence check on the frozen inputs, with fixtures that exercised only the zero-variance branches. Validation now: the within-law Beta-binomial check (largest error 2.0·10⁻⁶; acceptance 10⁻⁵), the fixed ρ̄_E = 0 binomial check (error 0; 10⁻⁹), the production rule against a four-fold finer rule on 45 frozen-input settings (the extremes of v, 7·10⁻⁹ to 3.02, and of the residual-scale ratio, 0.61 to 1.69, both evaluation sizes, both methods, every level, 40 random among 46,767 candidates: largest difference 6.9·10⁻⁵; acceptance 10⁻⁴) and on 120 fixed-threshold cases (4.5·10⁻⁶; 10⁻⁵), and Monte Carlo at the reviewer's two per-key examples and the fixed news example (every prediction within 1.2 SE; acceptance 4 SE + 10⁻⁴). (2) **F10:** the calibration contract recomputes every z summary from the committed array and checks κ̂, z_emp, z_pool and the fit detail; eight refusal fixtures (69 analyzer cases, 68 mutations refused, the complete pilot accepted). (3) **F11: correction to the round-1 entry above** — the finite-share fixture agreed to 10⁻⁴ (the acceptance then), not to 10⁻⁶ as written; the acceptance is now 10⁻⁵ with the largest observed error 2.0·10⁻⁶; §11 carries the generated finite-share paragraph; §9 states the share constants, every acceptance error, the empirical-cap slack and the −10⁻³⁰⁰ guard.

**Regenerated:** `INPUTS.json` (2,450 s; the round-2 file kept as `INPUTS_superseded_r2.json`): only the three `share_above_*_finite` fields and twelve timing fields differ; every moment, covariance, population share, mean, SD and theory-share prediction, v and order index equal; the Study 2 consistency, the v summary and the overlap unchanged. `VALIDATION.json` (run 20261015T191823Z; the round-2 file kept as `VALIDATION_superseded_r2.json`): all_ok = True; the cost probe 0.91 h projected (peak RSS 2.99 GB); eligibility unchanged (1,494 / 879 / 336 / 543). §11 regenerated.

**Round-3 prompt** (bounded to F9–F11) appended to `PROMPT.md`; the orientation's R07 row updated; `RESPONSE_claude_r2.md`. No judgement call for Yichen. Nothing verdict-dependent proceeds: no lock, no run.

**Skill lesson (A5, 2026-10-15):** a quadrature rule for an expectation of a sharp function (a binomial tail over a Gaussian shift) needs a convergence check on the production inputs' own settings, not only on the degenerate cases where a closed form exists; prefer an identity that makes the integrand smooth (here the Beta-cutoff form of the binomial tail) and put the production-setting convergence and a Monte Carlo check in the validator before the lock.

## 2026-10-16 (UTC) — Milestone 7: the design pilot, the record binding, the sizes; decision D12 (scope) put to Yichen

**The design pilot (validation on inputs; `research/study4/s4_pilot.py`; `outputs/study4_v0.1/pilot/pilot_summary_20261016T013804Z.json`; validation keys 1,000,311–1,000,312; 64 pilot prompts per setting; 256 new tokens; 26 settings; 21.9 minutes; process RSS 2.63 GB after the memory fix; no committed threshold read):** the vectorised loop at batch 32 runs at 428 tok/s unwatermarked (median), 357 / 333 / 283 tok/s with the KGW hook at h = 0 / 1 / 2 and 295–323 tok/s with the Gumbel hook. **The strength axis:** at δ = 0.5 the nominal power at α = 0.01 and the calibrated 256 tokens is intermediate (distinct rule 0.66 and 0.47, raw rule 0.73 and 0.45 for the two keys) at no visible quality cost (the model's perplexity of its own tokens 20.0–20.2 against 21.2 unwatermarked; the human continuations 11.2); at δ = 1 it is 1.00 at 256 tokens and 0.62–0.69 (distinct) at 64; at the published default δ = 2 it is 0.97 or more from 64 tokens on (perplexity 26.4–31.9); δ = 3 costs perplexity 41. **The key matters at a weak strength:** the two validation keys differ by 28 points of power at δ = 0.5 and 256 tokens under the raw rule (19 under the distinct rule; green shares 0.333 against 0.315). **Gumbel-max with the record's widths loops:** the share of texts with rep-4 above 0.5 is 0.97–1.00 at h = 0 and h = 1 and 0.42–0.52 at h = 2 (perplexity 1.3–6.9), where every KGW setting stays at 0.02 or below; so the record's Gumbel thresholds have no usable generation side at h ≤ 2 (a longer context would be a different detector than the one calibrated). **Chinese Wikipedia prompts:** KGW at δ = 2 reaches 0.95 or more from 64 tokens (perplexity 54.0 against 33.8 unwatermarked); Gumbel loops in 0.78 of texts. The table and its reading are §7 of the design notes (every number generated from the summary file by the notes' fill script).

**The record binding (`research/study4/s4_record.py`):** the only path through which Study 4 reads a committed threshold or a stored null level: it verifies the 240 record files against the run manifest's digests and recomputes the inventory digest (`c270ab81…`) with the manifest's own formula, checks the lock's digest (`0e288928…`) and the key record against the grid and `s3_keys`, requires every unit record to be a STUDY record under that lock with no FIXTURE marker and every per-key array to have exactly 1,000 finite non-negative entries, binds index i to evaluation key 23,000 + i, and marks zero thresholds as abstaining without ever repairing one. Its stored-null reader gives each key's realised level under every threshold from the evaluation units' counts (qwen news h = 1, distinct: nominal key-mean 1.12%, SD 0.61 pp; conformal@5000 1.00%, SD 0.17 pp — read, not retyped, by the self-test). Four refusal fixtures on scratch copies (a tampered threshold, a relabelled record, a missing file, a lock digest mismatch) all refused.

**Sizes priced at the measured rates (`research/study4/s4_sizes.py`; `outputs/study4_v0.1/sizes_options.txt`):** S (2 English cells; 100 keys × 50 prompts; KGW h = 1 at δ 0.5 and 2.0; 5,000 null texts per cell) 5.9 h; **M (recommended; 3 English cells; 100 keys × 50 prompts; h = 1 at both strengths and h = 2 at δ = 0.5; 5,000 null texts per cell) 12.7 h**; M+ (100 prompts per key) 22.8 h; L (200 keys, a Chinese cell) 57.6 h. Nominal precision at 100 keys × 50 prompts and power 0.7: per-key SE 0.065; the key-mean's SE 0.008–0.014; the between-key SD's relative SE 0.09–0.19 depending on the dispersion.

## 2026-10-16 (UTC) — Decision D12 (Yichen): Study 4's scope — option A, the power side and the model-null side with KGW alone

**Decision D12 (Yichen, 2026-10-16):** option A, the recommended one (the alternatives were B, the power side only, and C, a minimal one-cell design). Study 4 generates watermarked continuations with the KGW soft rule at h = 1 (the primary arm) and h = 2 (the design-remedy arm, paired on the same prompts and keys), scores them at every committed threshold of the key (every method, α and same-tokenizer calibration source of Study 3's record, both rules), and generates unwatermarked continuations from the same prompts, scored at every committed threshold for all 1,000 evaluation keys and both families (the deployer's false-positive rate on the model's own null law; the out-of-sample test of Study 3's key-free predictor on a non-corpus law). No Gumbel-max generation arm (the pilot's loops at h ≤ 2); the record's Gumbel thresholds receive a model-null level and no power side, stated as a limitation. Three English prompt cells: news, Wikipedia and Stack Exchange. Next: D13 (the model and its generations), then D14 (sizes).

## 2026-10-16 (UTC) — Decision D13 (Yichen): the model and its generations — Qwen2.5-1.5B alone at two KGW strengths

**Decision D13 (Yichen, 2026-10-16):** option 1, the recommended one (the alternatives were both models, adding Llama-3.2-1B at about double the generation hours, and a single strength). The generator is Qwen2.5-1.5B, the base model at the pinned revision `8faed761d45a…` (Apache-2.0; weights verified against the Hub digest); KGW at γ = 0.25 with δ = 0.5 (the pilot's weak strength, where the nominal power at the calibrated length is intermediate) and δ = 2.0 (the published default, saturated from 64 tokens); prompts of 64 tokens from fresh open units; temperature 1, no top-p or top-k; EOS and the special and padding ids excluded, so every text has exactly 256 generated tokens, with EOS's mass recorded per step; lengths 64, 128 and 256 by truncation, 256 the primary and calibrated length; the generated token arrays are the committed record (MPS bfloat16 generation is not promised bit-reproducible; everything downstream is recomputed from the arrays). The power side therefore covers the Qwen half of Study 3's record; the model-null block covers both families under that tokenizer. Next: D14 (sizes), priced at the pilot's measured rates within D12's scope (`research/study4/s4_sizes.py`; the M− option added for the decision).

## 2026-10-16 (UTC) — Decision D14 (Yichen): Study 4's sizes — option M

**Decision D14 (Yichen, 2026-10-16):** option M, the recommended one (the alternatives were M−, 60 keys at 8.6 h, and M+, 100 prompts per key at 22.8 h). K₄ = 100 evaluation keys, a seeded uniform sample without replacement of Study 3's 1,000 evaluation keys (the seed fixed in the grid before any generation with an evaluation key); 50 prompts per key, shared across keys (the first 50 production prompts of each cell); three English prompt cells (news/en@256, wikipedia/en@256, stackexchange/en@256); three watermarked arms per cell (KGW h = 1 at δ = 0.5, h = 1 at δ = 2.0, h = 2 at δ = 0.5), 45,000 watermarked texts; 5,000 unwatermarked texts per cell from 5,000 production prompts (the 50 watermarked prompts among them), 15,000 null texts, scored at every committed threshold for all 1,000 evaluation keys and both families; 12.7 h of generation at the pilot's measured rates (`outputs/study4_v0.1/sizes_options.txt`), to be re-projected by the validator from a production-scale probe of the runner itself before the lock. Next (no decision needed until R09): the grid and key subset, the production prompt cache, the scoring core, the runner and analyzer, the pre-lock inputs (the pilot law on validation keys), the validator and cost projection, the protocol draft, the re-screen, checkpoint R09.

**Two errors caught by the validator before any outcome (the conductor's):** (1) **F8 failed on the production prompt cache:** `build_prompt_cache` computed its plan with the pilot ids only when the pilot kind was requested, so the production scan of the first cache did not skip them and the news and Wikipedia pools contained some pilot prompts (the manifest's scan positions 15000–19999 and 15578–21640 against the plan's 15000–20019 and 15578–21650; Stack Exchange unaffected). Fixed: the pilot ids are always drawn and skipped; the first cache set aside as `prompts_manifest_superseded_pilot_overlap.json` and `prompts_superseded_pilot_overlap/` (never overwritten); the cache rebuilt. (2) **The analyzer refused the validation record** because its units carried the grid digest of the run time and the grid had been edited since (W1's and W2's completed pass fields): the provenance gate working as designed; the lesson is to generate the validation record after the last edit of the grid and code, which the chain now does (rebuild → the validation run → the validator). Both are the kind of thing the validation exists to find; neither touched a scientific input.
(3) **A third, in the same validation pass:** the validation grid asked the null unit for 100 texts from a pilot cache of 96 prompts, the runner silently produced 96 and the analyzer's body contract refused the unit. The runner now refuses a prompt range beyond its cache or different from the unit's size, the validation null unit is 96, and the validation record was regenerated under the final grid and code before the validator's final pass. Logged as the conductor's errors; every one found by the fixtures before any outcome, none touching a scientific input.
(4) **Two more corrections before the final validation pass, from reading the first complete `VALIDATION.json`:** the projection of the analyzer's output size had scaled the validation file by the key count, which its aggregate rows do not depend on (a planning figure of 571 MB where about three times the 7.3 MB validation file is right); corrected to scale with the prompt cells. And the validation record, with two keys and 96 null texts, cannot reach the agree, disagree, consistent or inconsistent branches of W2 and W3 (both W2 rows unresolved; all 80 W3 candidates ineligible at N₀ = 96), so the three rule-row computations were pulled out of `analyze` into `w1_row`, `w2_row` and `w3_fields` (behaviour unchanged) and the validator gained F9, which drives every branch with planted data (W1 consistent, inconsistent, unresolved; W2 agree and disagree on both predicted sides and the zero-denominator unresolved case; W3 consistent, inconsistent, unresolved, ineligible by theory share and by flag). The validator was re-run after these edits; the record it analyses was generated under the final grid and code.

## 2026-10-17 (UTC) — Milestone 8, during the run: the fixture generator and the report builder written and tested on a FIXTURE inventory (nothing read from the record)

**The R09 F18 side computation (labelled; not pre-registered; no locked output changed; `research/study1/twoway_se_check.py`, its generated memo `research/study1/TWOWAY_SE_CHECK_2026-10-18.md`, the JSON side files under `outputs/study1_v0.3/report/` and `outputs/study2_v0.1/report/`).** Three parts, every number in the memo generated. (1) The stored fields of every live cell and α of Studies 1 and 2 (3,582 and 10,404 (cell, α) rows; 3,180 and 9,724 with a positive SE and a non-zero estimate): the stored crossed bootstrap mean obeys the F18 identity (centred at (K − 1)/K · s_K²) on every row (the standardised discrepancy has SD 1.05 and 1.01 with 1%–99% quantiles about ±2.4 and 0.5% and 0.3% of rows beyond ±3); the displacement MS_E/N − s_K²/K is a median 1.3 and 2.2 crossed SEs (the reports' normal intervals were centred at the estimate, so it never entered a reported count); the stored crossed SE exceeds a key-only bootstrap SE from the stored per-key levels by a median factor 1.30 in both studies (5%–95%: 0.92–2.95 and 0.93–2.79), the excess largest where the across-key variance is small; with the key-only SE the reports' "within 2 SE" counts become 292 of 360 (Study 1, against 309 reported; the reported count reproduced from the cells with 0 mismatches against `results.json`) and 20 of 217 (Study 2, against 22), and 256 and 14 under the normal-theory SE. (2) Seven Study 1 cells rebuilt through the locked code (the same law, chain seed, production keys and colouring): the stored population blocks reproduced exactly (maximum relative difference 0.0 at every α) through the locked `cell_statistics` with the runner's per-cell seed; on the rebuilt tables the crossed bootstrap's mean sits at 0.995–1.001 of (K − 1)/K · s_K², the exact key-only SE agrees with the a_k-only approximation within 6%, the text-only channel is 5–15% of the key channel at α = 0.01, and the crossed-to-key SE ratio runs from 0.97 to 1.05 at α = 0.01 and up to 1.46 at α = 0.001. (3) A synthetic truth check at (K, N) = (500, 4000) and (1000, 10000) on three exact-target models (two Beta-Bernoulli key effects at Study 2's distinct and raw h = 1 scales; a logistic key-by-text model; 400 and 300 replicates): the estimate is unbiased (bias 0.00–0.11 sampling SDs); the crossed SE at the production B = 100 over-estimates the sampling SD of the estimate by 2–23% (coverage of v_K at nominal 95%: 0.93–0.98), the key-only SE sits at 0.93–1.02 of it (coverage 0.92–0.96), and the normal-theory SE under-estimates it (0.71–0.87; coverage 0.83–0.91) because the per-key levels are skewed. **Reading (the conductor's; judgement):** the crossed construction of Studies 1–2 is conservative and carries a text-duplication channel; the key-only SE (the construction Study 4 adopted at D17) is the one the paper reads those diagnostics with, both counts shown and labelled; the agreed reports are not regenerated; claim E1, constraint 25 and judgement J11 of the claim gate carry this. Run time: the stored part 90 s, the regeneration about a minute, the synthetic part 11 minutes; the first synthetic run (100 replicates at the large size) was repeated with 300 because two of its six empirical SDs sat two Monte Carlo SEs below their SE estimates (chance; the repeat confirmed it).

**The claim list (`research/CLAIM_GATE_v0.1.md` §1).** Drawn from the four agreed reports: A1–A7 (framing and theory), B1–B6 (the measurement on real null corpora), C1–C8 (remedies and transfer), D1–D9 (the power side), E1 (the secondary diagnostic), F1 (positioning), 1G (not claimed: the union of the reports' lists and the gate's constraints); each claim with its evidence table or section and its status (established / measured / hypothesis / attributed / narrowed); every number copied from a generated report and to be re-checked by the reviewer; the paper quotes numbers only through the builders' registers.

**The source gate re-run (§2–§4, §7).** Three delegated screens, launched a few minutes apart (A: theory, simulation and measurement; B: remedies, transfer, power and software; C: industry, regulators, standards and deployed systems), each handed the complete known record, the claims as items, the fallbacks, the pacing and the do-nots, each writing its memo and raw outputs under `research/claim_gate/` (`search_A/B/C_2026-10-18.md`; `raw_A/` 143 files, `raw_B/` 124, `raw_C/` 16). The arXiv API answered throughout (no 429 on A's and B's queries; three Semantic Scholar keyword searches of A met 429 and were not completed, the citation lists were). The conductor then fetched eleven e-prints under the arXiv terms (`tools/fetch_arxiv_pdfs.py CLAIM …`, paced 4 s, hashed in the manifest with the gate tag `CLAIM`: Nemecek et al. 2608.20047, ChainMark 2607.18445, Ding et al. 2609.32678, LUNA 2606.00613, TI-WM 2610.14378, ByteMark 2610.14427, Bhattacharyya and Bonnerjee 2610.17655, Wang et al. 2607.05694, Unigram 2306.17439, Li Ma 2609.15657, Khachaturov et al. 2504.12229) and confirmed every decisive quotation by substring search with its PDF page. **Result: no occupant of any claim row; no known predecessor with a version dated after 2026-10-13; the verdict PASS.** Occupied parts added to the gate's list: the comparison of the model's own unwatermarked outputs with human text as two negative classes for an operating point, in aggregate (LUNA, pp.7–8, 15: the two protocols "agree within 0.003 AUROC"); Unigram's marginal-over-split Type I theorem (p.8, Theorem 3.3, "over only the randomness of G"); per-key reporting over three fixed keys on AUC (TI-WM, pp.1–2); same-pool empirical calibration under one key and the multiplicity over candidate tokenizers (ByteMark, pp.7–9); the pooled one-corpus recalibration and the key-rotation statement (ChainMark, pp.1, 16); reference-bank calibration under the idealised null (Ding et al., p.36); per-language and pooled calibration (Nemecek et al., p.4: the pooled-null global threshold "is our headline"; "key" three times, in background); the fixed-key statements of the vLLM documentation (the paragraph byte-identical in v0.31.0 and main; the page changed 2026-10-05, 10-12 and 10-15; v0.32.0 released 2026-10-18) and OpenAI's textGrain report (§3.2, p.6), and the regulators' duty to measure error rates (the EU Code of Practice on Transparency of AI-Generated Content, document 129555, final 10 June 2026, Measure 3.2, p.17: "such as the error rate of the detection"; NIST AI 100-4, p.12). **Two corrections of earlier readings (the conductor's; verified at the primary source):** (i) the Study 3 re-screen of 2026-10-13 said of Ding et al. 2609.32678 that its limitations note "other watermark keys would need a new reference bank"; that sentence is not in the PDF text and is withdrawn (the gate quotes only what the text says); (ii) the brief and gate v0.3 call document 129555 "the EU Code of Practice on marking and labelling of AI-generated content" (the Commission's news wording); the document is titled "Code of Practice on Transparency of AI-Generated Content" and the local copy carries that title; the paper cites the document's own title and id.

**The wording constraints 17–33 (§5)** beside gate v0.3's 1–16: every per-key cost with its conditions and no ranking (17); W2 as a stated design limit (18); priority on pricing per-key calibration and per-key operating points to Genhatharan 2026 and AutoMark (19); the multiplier arm under Proposition 1's prerequisite only (20); the two exceedance estimands always distinguished (21); the predictor's validity stated by block (22); H-length as registered (23); language and domain as correlations and ratios of sampled levels with Nemecek et al. cited (24); the secondary diagnostic read with both SEs (25); design labels not identities (26); a hash as computational independence (27); side computations labelled post-hoc (28); disclosed text/code discrepancies travelling with their results (29); the key sample as one correlated draw (30); AI use named, no venue (31); LUNA, TI-WM and ByteMark cited where the two null laws and the tokenizer axis are discussed (32); regulator and vendor sources as vocabulary only, by id and date, never compliance (33).

**The wording judgements J1–J11 (§6)** are Yichen's, put to him one at a time with the conductor's recommendation first and the alternatives: J1 the h = 0 raw-rule observation in the paper (recommended: one descriptive paragraph under constraints 3 and 16 with Study 4's model-null contrast); J2 the predictor's validity on real text (by block with the registered pass and the measured bias); J3 H-length and the lattice size (as registered with the three groups; one sentence on the lattice); J4 the tokenizer-overlap account and the Qwen2.5 hypothesis (one labelled post-hoc paragraph; one hypothesis sentence); J5 a lattice-tie side computation (not now); J6 the multiplier wording (three measured sentences under constraints 13, 14, 20); J7 tolerance validity (both estimands named); J8 the transfer sentence (with the predictor clause and the Nemecek attribution); J9 W2 (decided 2026-10-18: a design limit); J10 the positioning sentence (stands after the search); J11 the secondary diagnostic (both counts, labelled). Decisions are appended to §6 and logged as they are taken.

**Checkpoint R11 opened (2026-10-19 UTC):** `research/reviews/R11_claim-gate/PROMPT.md` (the scope: the claim gate memo, the three screens with their raw outputs, the manifest's `CLAIM` entries, the side computation with its memo and outputs; six questions: the claims against the reports, the claims against the literature with the quotations re-checked by substring search, the constraints and the judgements, the side computation re-derived and re-run in part, the process, an independent search); the orientation updated (§1 the Stage 7 sentence, §2 the claim-gate and side-computation rows, §5 the side computation's run commands and the fetch tool, §7 the R11 row). ## 2026-10-22 (UTC) — Decision S1 (Yichen): the publication history rebuilt from a fresh root in a new private repository; the superseded repository renamed, nothing deleted, no force-push

**Decision (Yichen, 2026-10-22 UTC): A.** The existing private repository `SHENSHENZYC/watermark-key-validity` (two superseded snapshot commits, `698575e` and `9731bd1`, nothing else) is renamed to `watermark-key-validity-superseded-snapshots` and stays private as an archive, deletable later on Yichen's word; the snapshot is rebuilt with `tools/make_public_snapshot.py --git --fresh` from a clean commit (every ref of the old history bundled beside the snapshot, its SHA-256 logged), the history check run, a new private `SHENSHENZYC/watermark-key-validity` created and the fresh history pushed, the repository confirmed private with Pages off, the check re-run against the remote.
